# DF — review round 5: isolated negative oracles

Branch: `plans/ea-w2-filesafe-20261104`. Review baseline: `79baed4be8b1de3770846647eb27e4f438a72081`. Assessed repair and compact proof: `4309f746955e76e73f43a95e1b51defa6a793325`. The following report/self-grade commit changes no assessed source.

R4-B1 is repaired in all nine families. Their overall prepared verdict stays NOT_READY because new FIX5-N2 remains outside the explicitly authorized check-11 scope. Membership stays ABSENT; native execution stays NOT_RUN. The same seven narrowed EventRecord retention questions remain pending for Jared. No new product, retention, admission or governance decision is made.

## Review round 5: findings and responses

- **R4-B1 — fixed (`4309f746955e76e73f43a95e1b51defa6a793325`). Where:** `Plans/filesafe_event_contract_fixtures.json:487`, `Plans/filesafe_event_authority_fixtures.json:7329`, `scripts/pm_filesafe_guard_isolation.py:341` and `reports/event-authority-20261104/DF.guard-removal.json:4`. **Why:** unrelated execution-context deletions, legacy scope shapes, stale source commitments, receipt digests/full-value commitments and issued/boundary copies masked each intended negative. **Fix:** start from own-family valid controls, make one semantic defect, and recompute every unrelated body/source/receipt/issued/boundary copy. Required scope, execution context and legacy scope shapes are separate cases. Application originals use coherent declared/encoded values, genuine hash-addressed unused blobs and coherent manifest/outcome copies. Journal kind/subject cases retain typed operation fields, and missing-tombstone cases start from an actual valid delete. Protocol stage probes use real successful prefixes and coherent live/durable phases. **Verify:** both unmodified entry points, the literal guard-removal command below and the four full-CLI guard-removal controls. No finding is disputed.

Production payload, authority, schema-resolution and Storage digest code, all owner prose and selected schemas/resource graphs are byte-identical to the reviewed baseline. The new script is an independent read-only test driver; its mutants exist only in memory. No existing guard is weakened.

## Guard-removal proof and its layers

`python3 scripts/pm_filesafe_guard_isolation.py --jobs 8 --progress --output <evidence-path>` completed the literal matrix: **830 guards × 3119 negative controls = 2,588,770 executions**. Every declared target flips to acceptance at its stated boundary. Every other control stays rejected for its own reason; related upper-layer controls that depend on the same removed predicate are explicitly included in that guard’s flip set. There are **zero uncovered guard sites, unexplained flips or exceptions**. No trace-invariance shortcut was used for the delivered matrix.

The table has 824 single-removal rows and 6 explicitly named duplicate-group rows.

Schema controls execute their complete selected ingress root before and after guard removal, including the other-negative matrix. A schema result is reused only when every selected resource for that family is unchanged; affected registries are revalidated, including alternate union branches. Domain joins, constrained primitive fields, nullable results and canonical decoder token boundaries have explicit own-layer positive/negative controls. Complete materialization, retained-issued helpers, payload resolution and protocol steps are labelled separately. A downstream refusal or Python exception is never counted as acceptance of a full method. The named duplicate-guard rows additionally remove only the listed duplicate defenses together and require complete-boundary acceptance. The table explicitly lists their members. Reference-availability controls prove removal of the refusal signal only; they make no complete decoded-value claim.

The three materialization predicates singled out by the review also have full-checker controls: removing only thread join, current owner epoch or full-value commitment accepts all nine corresponding coherent negatives, and the actual authority CLI exits 1/status fail because it reports those accepted negatives. Removing only required(scope) accepts the nine actual repaired missing-scope cases and makes the payload CLI exit 1/status fail. The separate integration safety checks remain enabled.

All 131 original authority positives and 1170 original protocol traces are retained verbatim as data. All original negative IDs remain. The supplied authority pack now has 1731 negatives. The payload pack has 94 positives and 198 negatives; its original positives and identity vectors are retained, with valid outcome controls added. Long crash/recovery/withdrawal traces remain integration checks; the independent guard controls provide the check-11 proof.

## Astra defect-class mutation checks

| Defect class | Mutation and observed boundary |
|---|---|
| 1 — forged/contradictory inputs | Own identity, selected source, accepted-source/body/receipt/full-value commitments, application record/manifest/result joins and contradictory journal controls refuse. Literal guard removals expose their own negatives. |
| 2 — Storage-assigned metadata | Receipt event/sequence/storage/partition/durability changes refuse. Storage-only incoming retry metadata still returns the exact original issued value; caller-semantic changes conflict. |
| 3 — history in both directions | Retained originals pass after epoch changes and disposed controls; withdrawn/forged reads refuse. Added-history CLI control passes. |
| 4 — inconsistent representations | Actual typed ingress rejects numeric digest values, uppercase/spaced hashes and string/structured epoch values; LF and descriptor-canonicality guards have removal proofs. |
| 5 — unused dangling refs | Both entry points fail on rehashed unused dangling definitions; all 45 original closure negatives flip under resolver removal. |
| 6 — partial size checks | Exact ASCII/UTF-8 limits, oversized original/issued bodies and large SP-337 routes retain separate measured checks; missing/non-durable/changed packs refuse. |
| 7 — path custody | Missing selected source, original issued custody and first receipt are exercised at materialization, advancement, recovery, terminal, retry and withdrawal/publication boundaries. |
| 8 — masked negatives | Coherent fixture rebuilding plus the full literal table and named duplicate-guard removals establish each declared boundary; full-CLI controls cover the review counterexamples. |
| 9 — overstated grades | C12 stays PARTIAL because FIX5-N2 is accepted. C1 stays ABSENT; seven C7 decisions stay PARTIAL; all native behavior stays NOT_RUN. Each cell has refreshed exact citations. |
| 10 — numeric spellings | Actual original decoders reject JSON 1.0, 1e0, -0 and 01, MessagePack floating/non-shortest encodings; canonical integer controls pass. Storage JCS/MessagePack encoders reject floating values. Application epoch numeric hashing has the FIX5-N2 counterexample below. |
| 11 — type confusion | String booleans/numbers, null, arrays and objects at typed numeric/boolean boundaries refuse. Inline string/array/null values refuse before custody reads. The 386 representation probes pass. |

## FIX5-N2 — new numeric-epoch counterexample; outside authorized scope

**Where:** `scripts/pm_filesafe_event_authority.py:52`, `scripts/pm_filesafe_event_authority.py:65`, `scripts/pm_projects_refresh_contracts.py:129`, and the nine own Source schemas. **Why:** JSON Schema integer permits 1.0 and Python epoch equality treats it as 1; application semantic hashing encodes integer and float as different typed trees. Starting from each valid application case, changing only writer_epoch to 1.0 and recomputing its accepted-source commitment plus selected source copy still passes complete materialization, with a different digest. Event, receipt and issued values remain byte-value unchanged. All nine families reproduce it. Appending these nine coherent counterexamples as negatives makes the actual authority CLI exit 1/status fail with exactly nine negative_accepted diagnostics; its original fixture bytes are restored afterward. **Fix needed:** enforce one numeric epoch representation before commitment and equality, with coherent alternative-number negatives. The explicit round-5 scope permits check-11 fixture/proof repair only, so no new production/schema/hash guard is added. This is a technical integration follow-up, not an eighth retention question. C12 remains PARTIAL. `numeric-epoch-probes.json` records every own-family counterexample and SHA-256 custody below.

## Seven retention questions — unchanged

SP-552's authority-indefinite assignment and SP-556's non-authority operational assignment remain justified by current Case L-3 (`Plans/storage-plan.md:17403–17417`) and the exact existing event kinds. The remaining questions, copied from their current own SP paragraphs, remain unanswered:

| Family | Pending question |
|---|---|
| `filesafe.command_denied` | Does the retained command-denial EventRecord constitute audit authority (RP-AUTHORITY-INDEFINITE), or non-authority security diagnostic history (RP-OPERATIONAL-2555D)? |
| `filesafe.destructive_override_denied` | Does the retained destructive-override refusal EventRecord constitute an authority decision receipt (RP-AUTHORITY-INDEFINITE), or non-authority security diagnostic history (RP-OPERATIONAL-2555D)? |
| `filesafe.destructive_override_requested` | Does the retained destructive-override request EventRecord itself constitute approval/audit authority (RP-AUTHORITY-INDEFINITE), or non-authority security diagnostic history (RP-OPERATIONAL-2555D)? |
| `filesafe.guard_init_failed` | Does the retained fail-closed guard-initialization EventRecord constitute audit authority (RP-AUTHORITY-INDEFINITE), or non-authority security diagnostic history (RP-OPERATIONAL-2555D)? |
| `filesafe.path_denied` | Does the retained path-denial EventRecord constitute audit authority (RP-AUTHORITY-INDEFINITE), or non-authority security diagnostic history (RP-OPERATIONAL-2555D)? |
| `safe_point.created` | Is safe_point.created EventRecord history an authority/capture receipt (RP-AUTHORITY-INDEFINITE), non-authority recovery operational history (RP-OPERATIONAL-2555D), or runtime history (RP-RUNTIME-365D), independently of the already-governed released snapshot bodies? |
| `safe_point.restored` | Is safe_point.restored EventRecord history an authority/outcome receipt (RP-AUTHORITY-INDEFINITE), non-authority recovery operational history (RP-OPERATIONAL-2555D), or runtime history (RP-RUNTIME-365D), independently of the already-governed original restore transaction and snapshot bodies? |

These concern the separate retained EventRecords. Existing original input, snapshot, journal, body, generic index and checkpoint owners/lifetimes remain governed by their own contracts.
